MBA Semester II – SPPU

NEW SIMPLE VERSION
  • Every topic explained in easy words
  • Box-and-arrow diagrams
  • Examples from normal workplace situations
  • Shortcuts and memory codes
  • Answers for 2, 5 and 10/15 marks
  • All important questions answered unit-wise

Study Formula: Meaning → Diagram → Points → Example → Conclusion

HOW TO WRITE ANSWERS BY MARKS

2 Marks: Definition + 2 key points.
5 Marks: Definition + small diagram + 5 points + example.
10/15 Marks: Introduction + definition + diagram + 8–10 explained points + example + conclusion.

Meaning of Human Resource Management
SIMPLE MEANING
HRM = Getting the right people + Developing them + Motivating them + Retaining them.
EASY DIAGRAM
Plan people
→
Recruit & select
→
Train & appraise
→
Pay & motivate
→
Retain
→
Organization success
SHORTCUT / TRICK
Read: Meaning → Points → Diagram → Example.
SIMPLE EXAMPLE
Simple Example: A company estimates that it needs 20 sales executives, recruits suitable candidates, trains them, fixes performance targets, pays incentives and retains the best performers. The complete process is HRM.

        Show full explanation from the earlier HTML
        Definition - Human Resource Management (HRM): HRM is the systematic process of acquiring, developing, motivating, compensating and retaining people so that individual and organizational goals are achieved.
        Simple Meaning: HRM = Getting the right people + Developing them + Motivating them + Retaining them.
        Basic HRM Flow Human Resource Planning ↓ Recruitment and Selection ↓ Training and Performance ↓ Compensation and Employee Relations ↓ Retention and Organizational Success
        Simple Example: A company estimates that it needs 20 sales executives, recruits suitable candidates, trains them, fixes performance targets, pays incentives and retains the best performers. The complete process is HRM.
        Nature and Features of HRM
        SIMPLE MEANING
        Nature and Features of HRM explained in easy words using the points below.
        EASY DIAGRAM
        People-oriented
        →
        Pervasive
        →
        Continuous
        →
        Goal-oriented
        →
        Development-oriented
        SHORTCUT / TRICK
        P-C-G-D-I-S-M-F-A = People, Continuous, Goal, Development, Integrative, Strategic, Multidisciplinary, Future, Action.
        KEY POINTS
        1. People-oriented: HRM deals with employees as valuable organizational resources.
        2. Pervasive: Every manager performs HR activities, not only the HR department.
        3. Continuous: Recruitment, training, appraisal and motivation continue throughout organizational life.
        4. Goal-oriented: HRM connects employee contribution with organizational objectives.
        5. Development-oriented: It improves employee knowledge, skills, attitude and potential.
        6. Integrative: It balances organizational goals with employee needs.
        7. Strategic: Modern HRM supports long-term business strategy and competitive advantage.
        8. Multidisciplinary: It uses knowledge from psychology, sociology, economics, law and management.
        9. Future-oriented: It prepares employees and leadership for future changes.
        10. Action-oriented: It focuses on practical policies and decisions rather than only record keeping.
        SIMPLE EXAMPLE
        Use a simple workplace example connected with the definition and points.

        Objectives of HRM
        SIMPLE MEANING
        Objectives of HRM explained in easy words using the points below.
        EASY DIAGRAM
        Societal
        →
        Organizational
        →
        Functional
        →
        Personal
        SHORTCUT / TRICK
        O-F-P-S: Organizational, Functional, Personal, Societal
        KEY POINTS
        1. Main Objective: Organizational effectiveness through capable and satisfied employees.
        2. Organizational objective: Help the organization achieve productivity, quality and growth.
        3. Functional objective: Maintain the HR department at an appropriate and useful level.
        4. Personal objective: Support employee career, income, recognition and development.
        5. Societal objective: Follow laws, ethics, equality and social responsibility.
        6. Place the right person in the right job.
        7. Develop employee competence through training.
        8. Build motivation, commitment and job satisfaction.
        9. Maintain sound employee and industrial relations.
        10. Control labour cost and improve productivity.
        SIMPLE EXAMPLE
        A hospital recruits nurses, trains them and creates fair shifts so patient service and employee satisfaction improve.

        Show full explanation from the earlier HTML

        Main Objective: Organizational effectiveness through capable and satisfied employees.

        • Organizational objective: Help the organization achieve productivity, quality and growth.
        • Functional objective: Maintain the HR department at an appropriate and useful level.
        • Personal objective: Support employee career, income, recognition and development.
        • Societal objective: Follow laws, ethics, equality and social responsibility.
        • Place the right person in the right job.
        • Develop employee competence through training.
        • Build motivation, commitment and job satisfaction.
        • Maintain sound employee and industrial relations.
        • Control labour cost and improve productivity.
        • Retain talented employees and reduce avoidable turnover.
        Four-Level Objective Model Societal Objectives ↓ Organizational Objectives ↓ Functional Objectives ↓ Personal Objectives
        Scope of HRM
        SIMPLE MEANING
        Scope of HRM explained in easy words using the points below.
        EASY DIAGRAM
        Procurement — HR planning, job analysis, recruitment, selection, placement and induction
        →
        Development — Training, management development, career planning and succession planning
        →
        Compensation — Wages, salaries, incentives, bonuses and employee benefits
        →
        Integration — Motivation, communication, participation, discipline and grievance handling
        →
        Maintenance — Health, safety, welfare, quality of work life and employee records
        SHORTCUT / TRICK
        S = Scope
        KEY POINTS
        1. Procurement — HR planning, job analysis, recruitment, selection, placement and induction
        2. Development — Training, management development, career planning and succession planning
        3. Compensation — Wages, salaries, incentives, bonuses and employee benefits
        4. Integration — Motivation, communication, participation, discipline and grievance handling
        5. Maintenance — Health, safety, welfare, quality of work life and employee records
        6. Separation — Retirement, resignation, termination, VRS and exit management
        7. Strategic HR — Talent management, HR analytics, culture, change and workforce planning
        SIMPLE EXAMPLE
        Use a simple workplace example connected with the definition and points.

        1. Procurement — HR planning, job analysis, recruitment, selection, placement and induction
        2. Development — Training, management development, career planning and succession planning

        Importance of HRM explained in easy words using the points below.
        EASY DIAGRAM
        Ensures availability of competent employees.
        →
        Improves productivity and quality of work.
        →
        Reduces labour turnover, absenteeism and conflict.
        →
        Builds employee morale, motivation and commitment.
        →
        Supports innovation, change and technology adoption.

        2. Improves productivity and quality of work.
        3. Reduces labour turnover, absenteeism and conflict.
        4. Builds employee morale, motivation and commitment.

        6. Creates a positive work culture.
        7. Helps legal compliance and ethical employment practices.
        8. Develops future managers and leaders.
        9. Improves employer image and talent attraction.
        10. Creates sustainable competitive advantage.

        Functions of HRM
        SIMPLE MEANING
        Functions of HRM explained in easy words using the points below.
        EASY DIAGRAM
        Planning
        →
        Organizing
        →
        Directing
        →
        Controlling
        →
        Procurement
        →
        Development
        →
        Compensation
        →
        Maintenance
        SHORTCUT / TRICK
        PODC + PDCIMS = Planning, Organizing, Directing, Controlling + Procurement, Development, Compensation, Integration, Maintenance, Separation.
        KEY POINTS
        1. A. Managerial Functions
        2. Planning: Estimating workforce needs and deciding HR policies.
        3. Organizing: Designing HR structure, roles and authority.
        4. Directing: Guiding, motivating, communicating and leading employees.
        5. Controlling: Comparing HR results with standards and taking corrective action.
        6. B. Operative Functions
        7. Procurement of employees.
        8. Training and development.
        9. Performance appraisal and performance management.
        10. Compensation and benefits.
        SIMPLE EXAMPLE
        HR plans staff, recruits candidates, trains employees, pays salaries and handles grievances.
        Write: Definition + 2 points

        Show full explanation from the earlier HTML

        A. Managerial Functions

        • Planning: Estimating workforce needs and deciding HR policies.
        • Organizing: Designing HR structure, roles and authority.
        • Directing: Guiding, motivating, communicating and leading employees.
        • Controlling: Comparing HR results with standards and taking corrective action.

        B. Operative Functions

        • Procurement of employees.
        • Training and development.
        • Performance appraisal and performance management.
        • Compensation and benefits.
        • Employee welfare, health and safety.
        • Industrial and employee relations.
        • HR records, HRIS and compliance.
        • Separation and exit management.

        C. Advisory Functions

        • Advising top management on workforce strategy.
        • Advising line managers on recruitment, discipline, performance and employee issues.
        Functions of HRM Managerial Functions: Planning - Organizing - Directing - Controlling + Operative Functions: Procurement - Development - Compensation - Integration - Maintenance - Separation
        Memory Trick: PODC + PDCIMS = Planning, Organizing, Directing, Controlling + Procurement, Development, Compensation, Integration, Maintenance, Separation.
        Role of the HR Manager
        SIMPLE MEANING
        Role of the HR Manager explained in easy words using the points below.
        EASY DIAGRAM
        Strategic partner — Aligns HR strategy with business goals.
        →
        Administrative expert — Creates efficient policies, records, payroll and systems.
        →
        Employee champion — Represents employee needs, welfare and engagement.
        →
        Change agent — Supports restructuring, technology and culture change.
        →
        Talent architect — Builds recruitment, learning and succession systems.
        SHORTCUT / TRICK
        S-A-E-C-T-C-A: Strategic partner, Admin expert, Employee champion, Change agent, Talent builder, Compliance, Analyst
        KEY POINTS
        1. Strategic partner — Aligns HR strategy with business goals.
        2. Administrative expert — Creates efficient policies, records, payroll and systems.
        3. Employee champion — Represents employee needs, welfare and engagement.
        4. Change agent — Supports restructuring, technology and culture change.
        5. Talent architect — Builds recruitment, learning and succession systems.
        6. Compliance guardian — Ensures adherence to labour and employment rules.
        7. Culture builder — Promotes values, ethics, inclusion and teamwork.
        8. HR analyst — Uses workforce data for better decisions.
        SIMPLE EXAMPLE
        During a new software rollout, HR arranges training, communicates changes and supports employees.

        HR Policies and HR Practices
        SIMPLE MEANING
        Policy tells what and why.
        EASY DIAGRAM
        Policy vs Practice
        →
        Nature — General guideline — Specific action or procedure
        →
        Purpose — Provides consistency and direction — Implements the policy
        →
        Example — Equal opportunity policy — Structured, bias-controlled selection process
        →
        Time horizon — Relatively stable — May change with technology and needs
        SHORTCUT / TRICK
        HPHP = HR, Policies, HR, Practices
        KEY POINTS
        1. Policy vs Practice
        2. Nature — General guideline — Specific action or procedure
        3. Purpose — Provides consistency and direction — Implements the policy
        4. Example — Equal opportunity policy — Structured, bias-controlled selection process
        5. Time horizon — Relatively stable — May change with technology and needs
        SIMPLE EXAMPLE
        Use a simple workplace example connected with the definition and points.
        Write: Definition + 2 points

        Policy tells what and why.

        1. Policy vs Practice
        2. Nature — General guideline — Specific action or procedure
        Write: Meaning + Diagram + 5 points + Example

        Meaning: Policy tells what and why.

        Policy vs Practice
        →
        Nature — General guideline — Specific action or procedure
        →
        Purpose — Provides consistency and direction — Implements the policy
        →
        Example — Equal opportunity policy — Structured, bias-controlled selection process
        →
        Time horizon — Relatively stable — May change with technology and needs

        2. Nature — General guideline — Specific action or procedure

        Strategic Human Resource Management (SHRM)
        SIMPLE MEANING
        SHRM = Business strategy and people strategy moving in the same direction.
        EASY DIAGRAM
        Business strategy
        →
        Required skills
        →
        HR strategy
        →
        HR practices
        →
        Business results
        SHORTCUT / TRICK
        B-S-H-R: Business strategy → Skills → HR practices → Results
        KEY POINTS
        1. Nature and Importance of SHRM
        2. Long-term and future-oriented.
        3. Integrates HR decisions with business decisions.
        4. Focuses on organizational performance and competitive advantage.
        5. Ensures the right skills are available for future strategy.
        6. Builds culture, leadership and change capability.
        7. Uses workforce measures and evidence for decisions.
        8. Creates consistency among recruitment, appraisal, rewards and development.
        9. Traditional HRM vs SHRM
        10. Focus — Day-to-day employee administration — Long-term business advantage
        SIMPLE EXAMPLE
        An e-commerce company plans to automate warehouses, so HR recruits data specialists and reskills supervisors.

        Definition - SHRM: Strategic Human Resource Management is the planned alignment of HR policies and practices with the long-term strategy of the organization.
        Simple Meaning: SHRM = Business strategy and people strategy moving in the same direction.

        Nature and Importance of SHRM

        • Long-term and future-oriented.
        • Integrates HR decisions with business decisions.
        • Focuses on organizational performance and competitive advantage.
        • Ensures the right skills are available for future strategy.
        • Builds culture, leadership and change capability.
        • Uses workforce measures and evidence for decisions.
        • Creates consistency among recruitment, appraisal, rewards and development.

        Show full explanation from the earlier HTML

        A. Harvard Model

        The Harvard Model is a stakeholder-oriented model. It states that HR decisions are affected by stakeholder interests and situational factors. HR policy choices then produce employee and long-term organizational outcomes.

        Harvard Model Stakeholder Interests + Situational Factors ↓ HR Policy Choices (Employee Influence, HR Flow, Rewards, Work Systems) ↓ HR Outcomes (Commitment, Competence, Congruence, Cost-effectiveness) ↓ Long-Term Individual, Organizational and Social Results
        4C Outcomes: Commitment - Competence - Congruence - Cost-effectiveness.

        B. Matching Model (Michigan Model)

        The Matching Model emphasizes a strong fit between business strategy, organization structure and HR systems.

        Matching Model Business Strategy and Organization Structure ↓ Selection → Performance Appraisal → Rewards → Development ↓ Improved Individual and Organizational Performance

        Harvard Model vs Matching Model

        BasisHarvard ModelMatching Model
        ApproachSoft, stakeholder and employee-orientedHard, strategic fit-oriented
        FocusBalancing multiple interestsMatching HR systems with business strategy
        Main outcomesCommitment, competence, congruence, cost-effectivenessPerformance through selection, appraisal, rewards and development
        ViewBroad and pluralisticManagerial and performance-centred
        UNIT 1 - QUICK REVISION
        TopicKey Point
        HRMAcquire, develop, motivate, compensate and retain people
        Main goalOrganizational effectiveness through capable and satisfied employees
        FunctionsManagerial + operative + advisory
        SHRMAlignment of HR strategy with business strategy
        Harvard ModelStakeholders → HR policies → 4C outcomes → long-term results
        Matching ModelSelection → appraisal → rewards → development matched with strategy
        Key challengeManaging talent, technology, diversity, change and expectations

        Q1. Define Human Resource Management and explain its objectives and functions.

        Introduction: Human Resource Management is a systematic approach to managing people so that organizational and individual goals are achieved.

        1. Objectives: Achieve organizational goals; maintain the HR function; support employee growth; meet social and legal responsibilities.
        2. Planning: Estimate future workforce requirements and frame suitable HR policies.
        3. Organizing: Design roles, authority and HR department relationships.
        4. Directing: Motivate, communicate, lead and guide employees.
        5. Controlling: Measure HR results and take corrective action.
        6. Procurement: Recruit, select, place and induct the right employees.
        7. Development: Train employees and develop future leadership.
        8. Compensation: Provide fair wages, incentives and benefits.
        9. Integration and maintenance: Build motivation, welfare, safety and healthy relations.
        10. Separation: Manage retirement, resignation and other exits.
        Example: For example, an IT company uses workforce planning, campus recruitment, training, performance-linked rewards and career paths to build and retain software talent.

        Conclusion: Thus, HRM converts employee capability into organizational performance while also protecting employee interests.

        Q2. Explain Strategic Human Resource Management and the Harvard and Matching Models.

        Introduction: SHRM links people-management decisions with long-term business strategy. It ensures that workforce capability supports the future direction of the organization.

        1. Strategic alignment: HR priorities are derived from business goals.
        2. Long-term focus: SHRM anticipates future skills, leadership and culture needs.
        3. Integration: Recruitment, training, appraisal and rewards support the same strategic direction.
        4. Competitive advantage: Unique employee capability is difficult for competitors to copy.
        5. Harvard Model: Considers stakeholders and situational factors; HR policy choices produce the 4C outcomes and long-term consequences.
        6. Matching Model: Matches selection, appraisal, rewards and development with strategy and structure.
        7. Comparison: Harvard is broader and stakeholder-oriented; Matching is more performance and fit-oriented.

        Conclusion: Both models show that HRM is not only an administrative activity; it is a central part of organizational strategy.

        2. Pervasive: Every manager performs HR activities, not only the HR department.

        9. Performance appraisal and performance management.

        Example: HR plans staff, recruits candidates, trains employees, pays salaries and handles grievances.

        Conclusion: The topic helps the organization use people effectively and achieve better results.

        Shortcut: PODC + PDCIMS = Planning, Organizing, Directing, Controlling + Procurement, Development, Compensation, Integration, Maintenance, Separation.

        2. Long-term and future-oriented.

        Business strategy
        →
        Required skills
        →
        HR strategy
        →
        HR practices
        →
        Business results
        1. Nature and Importance of SHRM
        2. Long-term and future-oriented.
        3. Integrates HR decisions with business decisions.
        4. Focuses on organizational performance and competitive advantage.
        5. Ensures the right skills are available for future strategy.

        Example: An e-commerce company plans to automate warehouses, so HR recruits data specialists and reskills supervisors.

        Q7. Explain the Harvard Model and Matching Model of HRM.

        2 Marks5 Marks10/15 Marks

        2 Marks Answer

        The Harvard Model is a stakeholder-oriented model. It states that HR decisions are affected by stakeholder interests and situational factors. HR policy choices then produce employee and long-term organizational outcomes.

        1. A. Harvard Model
        2. B. Matching Model (Michigan Model)

        Shortcut: 4C Outcomes: Commitment - Competence - Congruence - Cost-effectiveness.

        5 Marks Answer

        The Harvard Model is a stakeholder-oriented model. It states that HR decisions are affected by stakeholder interests and situational factors. HR policy choices then produce employee and long-term organizational outcomes.

        A. Harvard Model
        →
        B. Matching Model (Michigan Model)
        →
        Harvard Model vs Matching Model
        →
        Approach — Soft, stakeholder and employee-oriented — Hard, strategic fit-oriented
        →
        Focus — Balancing multiple interests — Matching HR systems with business strategy
        1. A. Harvard Model
        2. B. Matching Model (Michigan Model)
        3. Harvard Model vs Matching Model
        4. Approach — Soft, stakeholder and employee-oriented — Hard, strategic fit-oriented
        5. Focus — Balancing multiple interests — Matching HR systems with business strategy

        Example: Use a simple workplace example connected with the definition and points.

        Shortcut: 4C Outcomes: Commitment - Competence - Congruence - Cost-effectiveness.

        10/15 Marks Answer

        The Harvard Model is a stakeholder-oriented model. It states that HR decisions are affected by stakeholder interests and situational factors. HR policy choices then produce employee and long-term organizational outcomes.

        A. Harvard Model
        →
        B. Matching Model (Michigan Model)
        →
        Harvard Model vs Matching Model
        →
        Approach — Soft, stakeholder and employee-oriented — Hard, strategic fit-oriented
        →
        Focus — Balancing multiple interests — Matching HR systems with business strategy
        1. A. Harvard Model
        2. B. Matching Model (Michigan Model)
        3. Harvard Model vs Matching Model
        4. Approach — Soft, stakeholder and employee-oriented — Hard, strategic fit-oriented
        5. Focus — Balancing multiple interests — Matching HR systems with business strategy
        6. Main outcomes — Commitment, competence, congruence, cost-effectiveness — Performance through selection, appraisal, rewards and development
        7. View — Broad and pluralistic — Managerial and performance-centred
        8. HRM — Acquire, develop, motivate, compensate and retain people
        9. Main goal — Organizational effectiveness through capable and satisfied employees

        Example: Use a simple workplace example connected with the definition and points.

        Conclusion: The topic helps the organization use people effectively and achieve better results.

        Shortcut: 4C Outcomes: Commitment - Competence - Congruence - Cost-effectiveness.

              Definition - Human Resource Planning: HRP is the process of forecasting an organization's future demand and supply of employees and preparing action plans to remove shortages or surpluses.
              Simple Meaning: HRP = How many people? What skills? At what place? At what time?
              HRP Balance Future HR Demand ↘ Compare → Gap ↗ Future HR Supply ↓ Recruit / Train / Transfer / Redeploy / Reduce
              Objectives, Need and Importance of HRP

              Avoid manpower shortage and surplus.
              →

              →
              Prepare succession and replacement plans.

              7. Improve utilization of existing employees.

              10. Provide a base for HR budgets and policies.

              Human Resource Planning Process
              SIMPLE MEANING
              Human Resource Planning Process explained in easy words using the points below.
              EASY DIAGRAM
              Business plan
              →
              Demand forecast
              →
              Supply forecast
              →
              Gap
              →
              HR action
              →
              Review
              SHORTCUT / TRICK
              O-I-D-S-G-A-I-R = Objectives, Inventory, Demand, Supply, Gap, Action, Implement, Review.
              KEY POINTS
              1. Study organizational objectives and business plans.
              2. Analyze the current workforce through HR inventory and skill inventory.
              3. Forecast future human resource demand.
              4. Forecast internal and external human resource supply.
              5. Compare demand with supply and identify gaps.
              6. Prepare action plans: recruitment, training, redeployment, succession or downsizing.
              7. Implement the plan.
              8. Monitor, review and revise the HR plan.
              SIMPLE EXAMPLE
              A bank opening 20 branches estimates how many managers and officers it will need next year.
              Write: Definition + 2 points

              Human Resource Planning Process explained in easy words using the points below.

              1. Study organizational objectives and business plans.
              2. Analyze the current workforce through HR inventory and skill inventory.

              Conclusion: Therefore, barriers to effective hrp helps improve employee effectiveness and organizational performance.

              Show full explanation from the earlier HTML
              • Uncertain business environment.
              • Inaccurate or incomplete employee data.
              • Weak support from top management.
              • Lack of coordination between HR and line managers.
              • Rapid change in skills and technology.
              • Resistance to workforce changes.
              • Overemphasis on numbers and neglect of quality.
              • Difficulty in forecasting employee behaviour and turnover.

                    Definition - Job Analysis: Job analysis is the systematic collection and study of information about a job's tasks, duties, responsibilities, working conditions and human requirements.
                    Simple Meaning: Job analysis studies the job, not the person.
                    Outputs of Job Analysis Job Analysis ├── Job Description: What the job contains └── Job Specification: What the job holder must possess
                    Job Analysis Process
                    SIMPLE MEANING
                    Job Analysis Process explained in easy words using the points below.
                    EASY DIAGRAM
                    Collect job data
                    →
                    Job description
                    →
                    Job specification
                    →
                    HR decisions

                    P-S-C-V-W: Purpose, Select jobs, Collect data, Verify, Write JD/JS
                    KEY POINTS
                    1. Decide the purpose of job analysis.
                    2. Select the jobs to be analyzed.
                    3. Collect background information and organization charts.
                    4. Choose data collection methods.
                    5. Collect job data from employees, supervisors and records.
                    6. Verify the information.
                    7. Prepare job description and job specification.
                    8. Update the information when work changes.

                    Methods of Job Analysis
                    SIMPLE MEANING
                    Methods of Job Analysis explained in easy words using the points below.
                    EASY DIAGRAM
                    Collect job data
                    →
                    Job description
                    →
                    Job specification
                    →
                    HR decisions
                    SHORTCUT / TRICK
                    O-I-Q-D-C: Observation, Interview, Questionnaire, Diary, Conference
                    KEY POINTS
                    1. Observation — Analyst watches the employee; suitable for visible and repetitive work.
                    2. Interview — Employee and supervisor explain tasks and requirements.
                    3. Questionnaire — Standard forms collect information from many employees.
                    4. Diary/Log — Employee records activities and time spent.
                    5. Technical conference — Experts and supervisors provide job information.
                    6. Critical incident — Important effective and ineffective behaviours are recorded.
                    7. Combination method — Two or more methods are used for better accuracy.

                    Tasks + responsibility + relationships
                    →
                    Productive and meaningful job
                    1. Factors Affecting Job Design
                    2. Organizational objectives and workflow.
                    3. Technology and equipment.
                    4. Employee skills and abilities.
                    5. Ergonomics, health and safety.

                    Show full explanation from the earlier HTML
                    Definition - Job Design: Job design is the process of arranging tasks, responsibilities and work relationships to make a job productive and meaningful.
                    Simple Meaning: Job design decides how work will be performed.

                    Factors Affecting Job Design

                    • Organizational objectives and workflow.
                    • Technology and equipment.
                    • Employee skills and abilities.
                    • Ergonomics, health and safety.
                    • Motivation and job satisfaction.
                    • Work group and social needs.
                    • Legal and environmental requirements.

                    Major Job Design Techniques

                    TechniqueMeaning
                    Job simplificationBreaks work into small standardized tasks.
                    Job rotationMoves employees between jobs to increase exposure.
                    Job enlargementAdds more tasks at the same level; horizontal expansion.
                    Job enrichmentAdds authority, responsibility and challenge; vertical expansion.
                    Autonomous teamsA team controls a complete work process.
                    Flexible work designUses flexitime, remote work or compressed work weeks.

                    Job Enlargement vs Job Enrichment

                    BasisJob EnlargementJob Enrichment
                    NatureAdds number and variety of tasksAdds responsibility and decision authority
                    ExpansionHorizontalVertical
                    Main aimReduce monotonyIncrease motivation and ownership
                    Skill impactUses similar-level skillsUses higher-level skills
                    ExampleCashier also handles returnsCashier plans counter schedule and resolves issues

                    Definition - Recruitment: Recruitment is the process of searching for and attracting a pool of qualified candidates for vacant jobs.
                    Simple Meaning: Recruitment creates applicants.
                    Recruitment Process Vacancy Identification → Job Requirements → Source Selection → Vacancy Communication → Applications → Preliminary Screening

                    Sources of Recruitment

                    Internal SourcesExternal Sources
                    PromotionCampus recruitment
                    TransferEmployment portals and company website
                    Internal job postingAdvertisements and social media
                    Employee databaseEmployment exchanges
                    Succession planConsultants and placement agencies
                    Former employeesEmployee referrals and walk-ins

                    Internal vs External Recruitment

                    BasisInternalExternal
                    Cost and timeLowerHigher
                    Employee motivationUsually improvesMay disappoint existing employees
                    New ideasLimitedGreater
                    RiskKnown performanceMore uncertainty
                    ChoiceLimited poolWider pool

                    Show full explanation from the earlier HTML
                    Definition - Selection: Selection is the process of evaluating applicants and choosing the most suitable person for the job.
                    Simple Meaning: Selection chooses the best candidate from the applicant pool.

                    Selection Process

                    1. Preliminary screening.
                    2. Application form and resume review.
                    3. Employment tests.
                    4. Selection interview.
                    5. Reference and background checks.
                    6. Medical examination where relevant.
                    7. Final selection decision.
                    8. Job offer and appointment.
                    9. Placement, induction and probation review.

                    Common Selection Tests

                    • Ability and aptitude tests.
                    • Achievement or job knowledge tests.
                    • Personality tests.
                    • Interest tests.
                    • Integrity tests.
                    • Work sample and simulation tests.
                    • Assessment centre exercises.

                    Types of Interview

                    • Structured interview.
                    • Unstructured interview.
                    • Panel interview.
                    • Sequential interview.
                    • Behavioural interview.
                    • Situational interview.
                    • Stress interview.
                    • Online/video interview.
                    Interview Quality Tip: Use the same job-related questions, a rating scale and trained interviewers to reduce bias.

                    Definition - Induction: Induction is the formal process of introducing a new employee to the organization, its culture, policies, people and job.
                    Simple Meaning: Induction helps the employee enter the organization smoothly.
                    Definition - Orientation: Orientation is the activity of familiarizing the employee with the immediate workplace, job, supervisor and colleagues.
                    Simple Meaning: Orientation is usually a part of induction.

                    Contents of an Induction Programme

                    • History, mission, products and organization structure.
                    • Employment terms, attendance, leave and code of conduct.
                    • Compensation, benefits and employee services.
                    • Health, safety and security.
                    • Department, job duties and performance expectations.
                    • Introduction to supervisor, team and key contacts.
                    • Technology, systems and required documentation.

                    Benefits

                    • Reduces anxiety and early turnover.
                    • Improves role clarity and productivity.
                    • Builds belonging and positive first impression.
                    • Reduces mistakes and safety risks.
                    • Helps faster cultural adjustment.

                    Definition - Employee Retention: Employee retention refers to the organization's ability and efforts to keep valuable employees for a desirable period.
                    Simple Meaning: Retention means keeping capable employees engaged and willing to stay.

                    Why Employees Leave

                    • Unfair pay or limited growth.
                    • Poor supervisor relationship.
                    • Work overload and stress.
                    • Lack of recognition.
                    • Weak job fit or unclear role.
                    • Unhealthy culture or unfair treatment.
                    • Better external opportunities.
                    • Inflexible work and poor work-life balance.

                    Retention Strategies

                    • Fair and competitive compensation.
                    • Career growth, learning and internal mobility.
                    • Supportive leadership and manager capability.
                    • Recognition and meaningful rewards.
                    • Employee engagement and voice.
                    • Flexible work and well-being support.
                    • Fair performance management.
                    • Strong onboarding and realistic job preview.
                    • Positive culture, inclusion and respect.
                    • Stay interviews and timely correction of problems.
                    Retention Chain Good Job Fit + Fair Rewards + Growth + Supportive Manager ↓ Engagement and Commitment ↓ Higher Retention and Performance
                    UNIT 2 - QUICK REVISION
                    TopicKey Point
                    HRPForecast demand and supply of employees, identify gaps and act
                    Job analysisProduces job description and job specification
                    Job enlargementMore tasks at same level
                    Job enrichmentMore authority and responsibility

                    SelectionChoose the best applicant
                    InductionOrganization-wide introduction

                    Q1. Explain Human Resource Planning and its process.

                    Introduction: Human Resource Planning ensures that the organization has the right people with the right skills at the right time and cost.

                    1. Study objectives: Understand strategy, expansion, technology and productivity plans.
                    2. Current HR inventory: Analyze number, skills, age, experience, performance and potential of employees.
                    3. Demand forecast: Estimate future quantity and quality of employees needed.
                    4. Supply forecast: Estimate internal availability and external labour-market supply.
                    5. Gap analysis: Identify shortages, surpluses and skill gaps.
                    6. Action plans: Use recruitment, training, transfer, succession, outsourcing or workforce reduction.
                    7. Implementation: Assign responsibility, budget and time schedules.
                    8. Review: Compare results with plans and update forecasts.

                    Conclusion: Effective HRP reduces labour cost and ensures smooth execution of business strategy.

                    Q2. Explain Job Analysis, Job Description and Job Specification.

                    Introduction: Job analysis systematically studies the content and requirements of a job. Its two major outputs are job description and job specification.

                    1. Purpose: Establish role clarity and provide reliable HR information.
                    2. Data collection: Use observation, interview, questionnaire, diary and expert methods.
                    3. Job description: Records title, purpose, duties, responsibilities, reporting, conditions and standards.
                    4. Job specification: Records education, experience, knowledge, skills, abilities and personal requirements.
                    5. Difference: Description explains the job; specification explains the required person.
                    6. Uses: HR planning, recruitment, selection, training, appraisal, compensation and safety.
                    7. Updating: Documents must be revised when technology or workflow changes.

                    Conclusion: Correct job information is the foundation of nearly every major HR decision.

                    Q3. Explain recruitment and selection processes and distinguish between them.

                    Introduction: Recruitment attracts qualified applicants, while selection evaluates applicants and appoints the most suitable candidate.

                    1. Vacancy and job requirements: Confirm need and use job analysis information.
                    2. Recruitment sources: Choose internal and/or external sources.
                    3. Communication: Advertise the vacancy clearly and attract suitable applicants.
                    4. Screening: Remove clearly unsuitable applications.
                    5. Testing and interviews: Measure job-related ability, behaviour and fit.
                    6. Verification: Check references, background and medical fitness where relevant.
                    7. Final decision and offer: Select the best fit and issue appointment terms.
                    8. Induction: Introduce the new employee to the job and organization.
                    9. Difference: Recruitment is positive and pool-building; selection is evaluative and choice-making.

                    Conclusion: A valid, fair and structured recruitment-selection system improves performance and reduces early turnover.

                    2 Marks5 Marks10/15 Marks

                    2 Marks Answer

                    1. Ensure the right number and type of employees.
                    2. Avoid manpower shortage and surplus.

                    Shortcut: R-P-R-T: Right people, Place, Role, Time | NH = Need, HRP

                                Performance Appraisal Methods
                                SIMPLE MEANING
                                Performance Appraisal Methods explained in easy words using the points below.
                                EASY DIAGRAM
                                A. Traditional Methods
                                →
                                Ranking — Employees are ranked from best to worst.
                                →
                                Paired comparison — Each employee is compared with every other employee.
                                →
                                Grading — Employees are placed in predefined performance grades.
                                →
                                Forced distribution — A fixed percentage is placed in each rating category.
                                SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                R-R-C-C-M-B-360: Ranking, Rating, Checklist, Critical incident, MBO, BARS, 360°
                                KEY POINTS
                                1. A. Traditional Methods
                                2. Ranking — Employees are ranked from best to worst.
                                3. Paired comparison — Each employee is compared with every other employee.
                                4. Grading — Employees are placed in predefined performance grades.
                                5. Forced distribution — A fixed percentage is placed in each rating category.
                                6. Graphic rating scale — Traits or results are rated on a numerical scale.
                                7. Checklist — Rater marks statements that describe employee performance.
                                8. Critical incident — Important effective and ineffective behaviours are recorded.
                                9. Confidential report — Supervisor prepares a private report, often in public services.
                                10. Essay method — Supervisor writes a descriptive evaluation.
                                SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                A salesperson is rated against sales target, customer feedback and reporting quality.

                                A. Traditional Methods

                                MethodExplanation
                                RankingEmployees are ranked from best to worst.
                                Paired comparisonEach employee is compared with every other employee.
                                GradingEmployees are placed in predefined performance grades.
                                Forced distributionA fixed percentage is placed in each rating category.
                                Graphic rating scaleTraits or results are rated on a numerical scale.
                                ChecklistRater marks statements that describe employee performance.
                                Critical incidentImportant effective and ineffective behaviours are recorded.
                                Confidential reportSupervisor prepares a private report, often in public services.
                                Essay methodSupervisor writes a descriptive evaluation.

                                B. Modern Methods

                                MethodExplanation
                                Management by Objectives (MBO)Manager and employee jointly set measurable goals and review achievement.
                                BARSBehaviourally Anchored Rating Scales combine rating scales with specific job behaviours.
                                360-degree feedbackFeedback is collected from boss, peers, subordinates, customers and self.
                                Assessment centreSimulations, exercises and observations assess managerial potential.
                                Balanced scorecardUses financial, customer, process and learning measures.
                                Psychological appraisalAssesses intellectual, emotional and motivational potential.
                                Human resource accounting approachRelates employee value or contribution to organizational results.
                                Common Appraisal Errors
                                SIMPLE MEANING
                                Common Appraisal Errors explained in easy words using the points below.
                                EASY DIAGRAM
                                Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                →
                                Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                →
                                Leniency — Rater gives excessively high ratings.
                                →
                                Strictness — Rater gives excessively low ratings.
                                →
                                Central tendency — Most employees are rated near the middle.
                                SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                H-H-L-S-C-R: Halo, Horn, Leniency, Strictness, Central tendency, Recency
                                KEY POINTS
                                1. Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                2. Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                3. Leniency — Rater gives excessively high ratings.
                                4. Strictness — Rater gives excessively low ratings.
                                5. Central tendency — Most employees are rated near the middle.
                                6. Recency effect — Recent events receive excessive weight.
                                7. Primacy effect — Early impressions dominate.
                                8. Similarity bias — Rater favours employees similar to self.
                                9. Contrast error — Employee is compared with another employee rather than standards.
                                10. Stereotyping — Rating is affected by group-based assumptions.
                                SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                Use a simple workplace example connected with the definition and points.
                                Write: Definition + 2 points

                                Common Appraisal Errors explained in easy words using the points below.

                                1. Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                2. Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                Write: Meaning + Diagram + 5 points + Example

                                Meaning: Common Appraisal Errors explained in easy words using the points below.

                                Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                →
                                Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                →
                                Leniency — Rater gives excessively high ratings.
                                →
                                Strictness — Rater gives excessively low ratings.
                                →
                                Central tendency — Most employees are rated near the middle.
                                1. Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                2. Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                3. Leniency — Rater gives excessively high ratings.
                                4. Strictness — Rater gives excessively low ratings.
                                5. Central tendency — Most employees are rated near the middle.

                                Introduction: Common Appraisal Errors is an important part of HRM because it helps an organization manage employees in a planned and fair way.

                                Definition: Common Appraisal Errors explained in easy words using the points below.

                                Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                →
                                Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                →
                                Leniency — Rater gives excessively high ratings.
                                →
                                Strictness — Rater gives excessively low ratings.
                                →
                                Central tendency — Most employees are rated near the middle.
                                1. Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                2. Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                3. Leniency — Rater gives excessively high ratings.
                                4. Strictness — Rater gives excessively low ratings.
                                5. Central tendency — Most employees are rated near the middle.
                                6. Recency effect — Recent events receive excessive weight.
                                7. Primacy effect — Early impressions dominate.
                                8. Similarity bias — Rater favours employees similar to self.
                                9. Contrast error — Employee is compared with another employee rather than standards.
                                10. Stereotyping — Rating is affected by group-based assumptions.

                                Example: Use a simple workplace example connected with the definition and points.

                                Conclusion: Therefore, common appraisal errors helps improve employee effectiveness and organizational performance.

                                Show full explanation from the earlier HTML
                                ErrorMeaning
                                Halo effectOne positive quality influences all ratings.
                                Horn effectOne negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                LeniencyRater gives excessively high ratings.
                                StrictnessRater gives excessively low ratings.
                                Central tendencyMost employees are rated near the middle.
                                Recency effectRecent events receive excessive weight.
                                Primacy effectEarly impressions dominate.
                                Similarity biasRater favours employees similar to self.
                                Contrast errorEmployee is compared with another employee rather than standards.
                                StereotypingRating is affected by group-based assumptions.

                                Ways to Reduce Errors

                                • Use clear job-related standards.
                                • Train raters.
                                • Use multiple data sources.
                                • Record performance throughout the year.
                                • Conduct calibration meetings.
                                • Provide an appeal or review mechanism.

                                SIMPLE MEANING
                                Performance Appraisal vs Performance Management explained in easy words using the points below.
                                EASY DIAGRAM
                                Plan
                                →
                                Coach
                                →
                                Review
                                →
                                Improve
                                →
                                Repeat
                                SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                P-C-R-I: Plan, Coach, Review, Improve
                                KEY POINTS
                                1. Nature — Periodic event — Continuous process
                                2. Focus — Past performance — Past, present and future improvement
                                3. Responsibility — Mainly supervisor/HR — Manager and employee jointly
                                4. Coverage — Rating and decision — Goal setting, coaching, review, reward and development
                                5. Orientation — Evaluation — Development and strategic alignment
                                6. Frequency — Usually annual or half-yearly — Regular and ongoing
                                SIMPLE EXAMPLE

                                            2. Task/job analysis — What must be learned? — Duties, standards, required competencies

                                            Training Methods
                                            SIMPLE MEANING
                                            Training Methods explained in easy words using the points below.
                                            EASY DIAGRAM
                                            A. On-the-Job Methods
                                            →
                                            Coaching — Supervisor guides the employee during actual work.
                                            →
                                            Mentoring — Experienced person supports long-term growth.
                                            →
                                            Job rotation — Employee learns through movement across jobs.
                                            →
                                            Apprenticeship — Structured practical learning under an expert.
                                            SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                            OJT = Learn while working; Off-JT = Learn away from work
                                            KEY POINTS
                                            1. A. On-the-Job Methods
                                            2. Coaching — Supervisor guides the employee during actual work.
                                            3. Mentoring — Experienced person supports long-term growth.
                                            4. Job rotation — Employee learns through movement across jobs.
                                            5. Apprenticeship — Structured practical learning under an expert.
                                            6. Understudy — Employee learns as a potential replacement.
                                            7. Committee assignment — Employee develops through team problem-solving.
                                            8. Internship — Student or learner gains supervised work experience.
                                            9. Demonstration — Trainer shows the correct method at the workplace.
                                            10. B. Off-the-Job Methods

                                            A. On-the-Job Methods

                                            MethodExplanation
                                            CoachingSupervisor guides the employee during actual work.
                                            MentoringExperienced person supports long-term growth.
                                            Job rotationEmployee learns through movement across jobs.
                                            ApprenticeshipStructured practical learning under an expert.
                                            UnderstudyEmployee learns as a potential replacement.
                                            Committee assignmentEmployee develops through team problem-solving.
                                            InternshipStudent or learner gains supervised work experience.
                                            DemonstrationTrainer shows the correct method at the workplace.

                                            B. Off-the-Job Methods

                                            MethodExplanation
                                            LectureTrainer presents concepts to a group.
                                            Case studyParticipants analyze a real or simulated business problem.
                                            Role playParticipants act out workplace situations.
                                            SimulationReplicates job conditions without real risk.
                                            Vestibule trainingTraining occurs on similar equipment away from the actual work area.
                                            Management gamesTeams make decisions in a simulated business.
                                            Sensitivity trainingImproves self-awareness and interpersonal understanding.
                                            Conference/workshopParticipants discuss and practice concepts.
                                            Programmed instructionContent is delivered in sequenced self-learning units.
                                            E-learningDigital courses, virtual classrooms, videos and learning platforms.

                                            E-Learning
                                            SIMPLE MEANING
                                            Learning through online courses, virtual classrooms, mobile modules or learning management systems.
                                            EASY DIAGRAM
                                            Anytime-anywhere access.
                                            →
                                            Scalable and cost-effective for large groups.
                                            →
                                            Consistent content.
                                            →
                                            Self-paced learning.
                                            →
                                            Easy tracking and assessment.
                                            SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                            A-S-T: Anywhere, Self-paced, Trackable
                                            KEY POINTS
                                            1. Anytime-anywhere access.
                                            2. Scalable and cost-effective for large groups.
                                            3. Consistent content.
                                            4. Self-paced learning.
                                            5. Easy tracking and assessment.
                                            6. Quick updating of material.
                                            7. Requires technology and digital comfort.
                                            8. Lower personal interaction.
                                            9. Possible distraction and low completion.
                                            10. Not suitable for every physical skill.
                                            SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                            Employees complete a cybersecurity course through the company learning portal.
                                            Write: Definition + 2 points

                                            Learning through online courses, virtual classrooms, mobile modules or learning management systems.

                                            Definition - E-Learning: E-learning uses digital technology to deliver, support and track learning.
                                            Simple Meaning: Learning through online courses, virtual classrooms, mobile modules or learning management systems.

                                            Advantages

                                            • Anytime-anywhere access.
                                            • Scalable and cost-effective for large groups.
                                            • Consistent content.
                                            • Self-paced learning.
                                            • Easy tracking and assessment.
                                            • Quick updating of material.

                                            Limitations

                                            • Requires technology and digital comfort.
                                            • Lower personal interaction.
                                            • Possible distraction and low completion.
                                            • Not suitable for every physical skill.
                                            • Quality depends on design and learner motivation.

                                            UNIT 3 - QUICK REVISION
                                            TopicKey Point

                                            Performance managementContinuous goal, coaching, review and development cycle

                                            Q1. Explain performance appraisal process and major appraisal methods.

                                            Introduction: Performance appraisal systematically evaluates employee performance against job-related standards and supports feedback, rewards and development decisions.

                                            1. Set standards: Define expected results, competencies and behaviours.
                                            2. Communicate: Ensure employees understand goals and measures.
                                            3. Measure: Collect evidence through observation, records and results.
                                            4. Compare: Compare actual performance with standards.
                                            5. Discuss: Conduct a two-way appraisal interview.
                                            6. Action: Decide recognition, development or performance improvement.
                                            7. Traditional methods: Ranking, rating scales, checklist, critical incident and confidential report.
                                            8. Modern methods: MBO, BARS, 360-degree feedback and assessment centre.
                                            9. Control errors: Train raters and use multiple sources and clear standards.

                                            Conclusion: A fair appraisal system should improve performance, not merely produce a score.

                                            Q2. Explain training and development process and training methods.

                                            Introduction: Training improves present-job performance, while development builds long-term capability. A systematic training process begins with need assessment and ends with evaluation and transfer of learning.

                                            1. Need assessment: Analyze organization, job and person gaps.
                                            2. Objectives: State measurable learning outcomes.
                                            3. Design: Select content, trainer, method, schedule and resources.
                                            4. Delivery: Conduct learning with practice and participation.
                                            5. On-the-job methods: Coaching, mentoring, rotation, apprenticeship and understudy.
                                            6. Off-the-job methods: Lecture, case, role play, simulation, vestibule and e-learning.
                                            7. Evaluation: Measure reaction, learning, behaviour and results.
                                            8. Follow-up: Provide supervisor support so learning is used on the job.

                                            Conclusion: Training becomes valuable only when acquired learning improves workplace behaviour and organizational results.

                                            Q3. Explain the Kirkpatrick Model of training evaluation.

                                            Introduction: The Kirkpatrick Model evaluates training at four progressively stronger levels.

                                            1. Reaction: Measures learner satisfaction, engagement and perceived relevance.
                                            2. Learning: Measures knowledge, skills, attitude or confidence gained.
                                            3. Behaviour: Checks whether learning is applied in actual work.
                                            4. Results: Measures impact on productivity, quality, sales, cost, safety or other business outcomes.
                                            5. Use: Organizations should define desired results before training and collect evidence at each level.
                                            6. Limitation: Higher-level results may be affected by factors other than training, so careful measurement is required.

                                            Conclusion: The model helps HR move from simply counting participants to proving whether training created useful organizational change.

                                            Q3. Discuss common appraisal errors and ways to reduce them.

                                            2 Marks5 Marks10/15 Marks

                                            2 Marks Answer

                                            Common Appraisal Errors explained in easy words using the points below. Performance appraisal judges how well an employee performed during a period.

                                            1. Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                            2. Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.

                                            Shortcut: H-H-L-S-C-R: Halo, Horn, Leniency, Strictness, Central tendency, Recency | PA = Performance, Appraisal

                                            5 Marks Answer

                                            Common Appraisal Errors explained in easy words using the points below. Performance appraisal judges how well an employee performed during a period.

                                            Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                            →
                                            Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                            →
                                            Leniency — Rater gives excessively high ratings.
                                            →
                                            Strictness — Rater gives excessively low ratings.
                                            →
                                            Central tendency — Most employees are rated near the middle.
                                            →
                                            Meaning
                                            1. Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                            2. Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                            3. Leniency — Rater gives excessively high ratings.
                                            4. Strictness — Rater gives excessively low ratings.
                                            5. Central tendency — Most employees are rated near the middle.

                                            Example: Use a simple workplace example connected with the definition and points.

                                            Shortcut: H-H-L-S-C-R: Halo, Horn, Leniency, Strictness, Central tendency, Recency | PA = Performance, Appraisal

                                            10/15 Marks Answer

                                            Common Appraisal Errors explained in easy words using the points below. Performance appraisal judges how well an employee performed during a period.

                                            Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                            →
                                            Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                            →
                                            Leniency — Rater gives excessively high ratings.
                                            →
                                            Strictness — Rater gives excessively low ratings.
                                            →
                                            Central tendency — Most employees are rated near the middle.
                                            →
                                            Meaning
                                            1. Halo effect — One positive quality influences all ratings.
                                            2. Horn effect — One negative quality lowers all ratings.
                                            3. Leniency — Rater gives excessively high ratings.
                                            4. Strictness — Rater gives excessively low ratings.
                                            5. Central tendency — Most employees are rated near the middle.
                                            6. Recency effect — Recent events receive excessive weight.
                                            7. Primacy effect — Early impressions dominate.
                                            8. Similarity bias — Rater favours employees similar to self.
                                            9. Contrast error — Employee is compared with another employee rather than standards.

                                            Example: Use a simple workplace example connected with the definition and points.

                                            Conclusion: The topic helps the organization use people effectively and achieve better results.

                                            Q8. Explain on-the-job and off-the-job training methods.

                                            Q9. Explain training need assessment.

                                            2 Marks5 Marks10/15 Marks

                                            2 Marks Answer

                                            Training Need Assessment (TNA) explained in easy words using the points below. Training prepares the employee for the present job.

                                            1. Organizational analysis — Where is training needed? — Strategy, productivity, customer complaints, technology changes
                                            2. Task/job analysis — What must be learned? — Duties, standards, required competencies

                                            Shortcut: O-T-P: Organization, Task, Person | TD = Training, Development

                                            5 Marks Answer

                                            Training Need Assessment (TNA) explained in easy words using the points below. Training prepares the employee for the present job.

                                            Organization analysis
                                            →
                                            Task analysis
                                            →
                                            Person analysis
                                            →
                                            Training need
                                            →
                                            Meaning
                                            →
                                            Key points
                                            1. Organizational analysis — Where is training needed? — Strategy, productivity, customer complaints, technology changes
                                            2. Task/job analysis — What must be learned? — Duties, standards, required competencies
                                            3. Person analysis — Who needs training? — Performance gaps, tests, appraisal data
                                            4. TNA Methods
                                            5. Performance appraisal reports.

                                            Example: Customer complaints show that service staff need communication training.

                                            Shortcut: O-T-P: Organization, Task, Person | TD = Training, Development

                                            Q10. Explain e-learning with advantages and limitations.

                                            2 Marks5 Marks10/15 Marks

                                            2 Marks Answer

                                            Learning through online courses, virtual classrooms, mobile modules or learning management systems.

                                            1. Anytime-anywhere access.
                                            2. Scalable and cost-effective for large groups.

                                            Shortcut: A-S-T: Anywhere, Self-paced, Trackable

                                            5 Marks Answer

                                            Learning through online courses, virtual classrooms, mobile modules or learning management systems.

                                            Anytime-anywhere access.
                                            →
                                            Scalable and cost-effective for large groups.
                                            →
                                            Consistent content.
                                            →
                                            Self-paced learning.
                                            →
                                            Easy tracking and assessment.
                                            1. Anytime-anywhere access.
                                            2. Scalable and cost-effective for large groups.
                                            3. Consistent content.
                                            4. Self-paced learning.
                                            5. Easy tracking and assessment.

                                            Example: Employees complete a cybersecurity course through the company learning portal.

                                            Shortcut: A-S-T: Anywhere, Self-paced, Trackable

                                            10/15 Marks Answer

                                            Learning through online courses, virtual classrooms, mobile modules or learning management systems.

                                            Anytime-anywhere access.
                                            →
                                            Scalable and cost-effective for large groups.
                                            →
                                            Consistent content.
                                            →
                                            Self-paced learning.
                                            →
                                            Easy tracking and assessment.
                                            1. Anytime-anywhere access.
                                            2. Scalable and cost-effective for large groups.
                                            3. Consistent content.
                                            4. Self-paced learning.
                                            5. Easy tracking and assessment.
                                            6. Quick updating of material.
                                            7. Requires technology and digital comfort.
                                            8. Lower personal interaction.
                                            9. Possible distraction and low completion.

                                            Example: Employees complete a cybersecurity course through the company learning portal.

                                            Conclusion: The topic helps the organization use people effectively and achieve better results.
