                                            Q11. Explain the Kirkpatrick Model.

                                            2 Marks5 Marks10/15 Marks

                                            2 Marks Answer

                                            Evaluation of Training - Kirkpatrick Model explained in easy words using the points below.

                                            1. 1. Reaction — How participants felt about training — Feedback form, rating survey
                                            2. 2. Learning — Increase in knowledge or skill — Pre/post test, demonstration

                                            Shortcut: R-L-B-R = Reaction, Learning, Behaviour, Results.

                                            5 Marks Answer

                                            Evaluation of Training - Kirkpatrick Model explained in easy words using the points below.

                                            Reaction
                                            →
                                            Learning
                                            →
                                            Behaviour
                                            →
                                            Results
                                            1. 1. Reaction — How participants felt about training — Feedback form, rating survey
                                            2. 2. Learning — Increase in knowledge or skill — Pre/post test, demonstration
                                            3. 3. Behaviour — Transfer of learning to the job — Observation, manager review, work evidence
                                            4. 4. Results — Organizational outcomes — Productivity, quality, sales, cost, safety, retention

                                            Example: After sales training, HR checks feedback, test scores, selling behaviour and actual sales growth.

                                            Shortcut: R-L-B-R = Reaction, Learning, Behaviour, Results.

                                            10/15 Marks Answer

                                            Evaluation of Training - Kirkpatrick Model explained in easy words using the points below.

                                            Reaction

                                            4. 4. Results — Organizational outcomes — Productivity, quality, sales, cost, safety, retention

                                            Compensation = Pay + Incentives + Benefits + Recognition.

                                            →
                                            Incentive
                                            →
                                            Benefits
                                            →
                                            Recognition
                                            →
                                            Total reward

                                                  Definition - Compensation: Compensation is the total financial and non-financial return provided to an employee in exchange for work and contribution.
                                                  Simple Meaning: Compensation = Pay + Incentives + Benefits + Recognition.
                                                  Total Compensation Direct Financial Pay (Basic salary, allowances, bonus, incentives) + Indirect Financial Benefits (Insurance, leave, retirement, welfare) + Non-Financial Rewards (Recognition, growth, autonomy, work environment)

                                                  Principles of Sound Compensation
                                                  SIMPLE MEANING
                                                  Principles of Sound Compensation explained in easy words using the points below.
                                                  EASY DIAGRAM
                                                  Internal equity
                                                  →
                                                  External competitiveness
                                                  →
                                                  Individual equity
                                                  →
                                                  Legal compliance.
                                                  →
                                                  Affordability and cost control.
                                                  SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                  F-A-I-R: Fair, Affordable, Internal/market fit, Rule-compliant
                                                  KEY POINTS
                                                  1. Internal equity: Comparable jobs receive comparable pay.
                                                  2. External competitiveness: Pay is reasonable compared with the labour market.
                                                  3. Individual equity: Differences reflect skill, performance or contribution.
                                                  4. Legal compliance.
                                                  5. Affordability and cost control.
                                                  6. Transparency and easy understanding.
                                                  7. Flexibility according to workforce and business needs.
                                                  8. Consistency and timely payment.
                                                  SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                                  An employee receives basic salary, bonus, insurance and recognition for good work.
                                                  Write: Definition + 2 points

                                                  1. Internal equity: Comparable jobs receive comparable pay.
                                                  2. External competitiveness: Pay is reasonable compared with the labour market.

                                                  Compensation Management Process
                                                  SIMPLE MEANING
                                                  Compensation Management Process explained in easy words using the points below.
                                                  EASY DIAGRAM
                                                  Job analysis
                                                  →
                                                  Job evaluation
                                                  →
                                                  Market survey
                                                  →
                                                  Pay structure
                                                  →
                                                  Incentives & benefits
                                                  →
                                                  Review
                                                  SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                  A-E-S-P-C-R: Analyze, Evaluate jobs, Survey market, Pay structure, Communicate, Review
                                                  KEY POINTS
                                                  1. Analyze organizational strategy and compensation philosophy.
                                                  2. Conduct job analysis.
                                                  3. Evaluate jobs and build job grades.
                                                  4. Study market pay through salary surveys.
                                                  5. Design pay structure and salary ranges.
                                                  6. Decide incentives and benefits.
                                                  7. Implement, communicate and administer the plan.
                                                  8. Review equity, cost, performance and market competitiveness.
                                                  SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                                  An employee receives basic salary, bonus, insurance and recognition for good work.

                                                  Components of Salary
                                                  SIMPLE MEANING
                                                  Components of Salary explained in easy words using the points below.
                                                  EASY DIAGRAM
                                                  Basic salary/wage — Core fixed payment for the job.
                                                  →
                                                  Dearness allowance — Allowance intended to offset changes in cost of living.
                                                  →
                                                  House rent allowance — Support toward housing cost.
                                                  →
                                                  Other allowances — Travel, education, shift, location or special allowances.
                                                  →
                                                  Bonus — Additional payment based on law, profit or performance.
                                                  SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                  B-A-B-I-B: Basic, Allowances, Bonus, Incentive, Benefits
                                                  KEY POINTS
                                                  1. Basic salary/wage — Core fixed payment for the job.
                                                  2. Dearness allowance — Allowance intended to offset changes in cost of living.
                                                  3. House rent allowance — Support toward housing cost.
                                                  4. Other allowances — Travel, education, shift, location or special allowances.
                                                  5. Bonus — Additional payment based on law, profit or performance.
                                                  6. Incentive/variable pay — Payment linked to results, output or targets.
                                                  7. Benefits/perquisites — Insurance, retirement, leave, vehicle, meals or other facilities.
                                                  8. Deductions — Authorized deductions such as tax or employee contributions.
                                                  SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                                  Monthly pay may include basic salary, HRA, allowances, incentive and deductions.

                                                  →
                                                  Differences in skill, education and experience.
                                                  →
                                                  Job difficulty, responsibility and risk.
                                                  →
                                                  Performance and productivity.
                                                  →
                                                  Labour-market scarcity.

                                                  →
                                                  Profit sharing — Participation in decision making

                                                  8. Team incentive — Status, awards and meaningful work

                                                  Employee Separation
                                                  SIMPLE MEANING
                                                  Employee separation is the ending of the employment relationship between an employee and the organization.
                                                  EASY DIAGRAM
                                                  Resignation / Retirement / Termination / Layoff
                                                  →
                                                  Exit process
                                                  →
                                                  Final settlement
                                                  SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                  R-R-T-L-D: Resignation, Retirement, Termination, Layoff, Dismissal
                                                  KEY POINTS
                                                  1. Forms of Separation
                                                  2. Resignation — Employee voluntarily leaves the organization.
                                                  3. Retirement — Employment ends after reaching retirement age or service conditions.
                                                  4. Voluntary Retirement Scheme (VRS) — Employee voluntarily accepts an organization-sponsored early-exit package.
                                                  5. Termination — Employer ends employment according to contract, policy or law.
                                                  6. Dismissal — Employment is ended as disciplinary punishment for serious misconduct.
                                                  7. Discharge — Employee is released for reasons other than major misconduct, subject to applicable rules.
                                                  8. Layoff — Temporary inability of the employer to provide work for specified reasons.
                                                  9. Retrenchment — Termination due to surplus workforce or restructuring, subject to law.
                                                  10. Death/disability — Employment ends because the employee can no longer continue.
                                                  SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                                  An employee may leave through resignation, retirement or termination.
                                                  Write: Definition + 2 points

                                                  Employee separation is the ending of the employment relationship between an employee and the organization.

                                                  1. Forms of Separation
                                                  2. Resignation — Employee voluntarily leaves the organization.
                                                  Write: Meaning + Diagram + 5 points + Example

                                                  Meaning: Employee separation is the ending of the employment relationship between an employee and the organization.

                                                  →
                                                  Exit process
                                                  →
                                                  Final settlement

                                                  5. Termination — Employer ends employment according to contract, policy or law.

                                                  Example: An employee may leave through resignation, retirement or termination.

                                                  Definition - Employee Separation: Employee separation is the ending of the employment relationship between an employee and the organization.

                                                  Forms of Separation

                                                  FormMeaning
                                                  ResignationEmployee voluntarily leaves the organization.
                                                  RetirementEmployment ends after reaching retirement age or service conditions.

                                                  DismissalEmployment is ended as disciplinary punishment for serious misconduct.

                                                  Death/disabilityEmployment ends because the employee can no longer continue.
                                                  End of contractFixed-term employment ends on completion of the agreed period or work.
                                                  VRS and Golden Handshake
                                                  SIMPLE MEANING
                                                  VRS is an arrangement under which eligible employees voluntarily retire before normal retirement in return for specified benefits.
                                                  EASY DIAGRAM
                                                  Reduce surplus workforce.
                                                  →
                                                  Support restructuring and technology change.
                                                  →
                                                  Reduce long-term employee cost.
                                                  →
                                                  Avoid compulsory separation where possible.
                                                  →
                                                  Risks
                                                  SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                  VRS = Voluntary exit + Retirement package
                                                  KEY POINTS
                                                  1. Reduce surplus workforce.
                                                  2. Support restructuring and technology change.
                                                  3. Reduce long-term employee cost.
                                                  4. Avoid compulsory separation where possible.
                                                  5. Risks
                                                  6. Loss of valuable experience.
                                                  7. Low morale among remaining employees.
                                                  8. Possibility that high performers leave.
                                                  9. Large immediate financial outflow.
                                                  SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                                  A company offers eligible employees an early-retirement package during restructuring.
                                                  Write: Definition + 2 points

                                                  VRS is an arrangement under which eligible employees voluntarily retire before normal retirement in return for specified benefits.

                                                  1. Reduce surplus workforce.
                                                  2. Support restructuring and technology change.
                                                  Write: Meaning + Diagram + 5 points + Example

                                                  Support restructuring and technology change.
                                                  →
                                                  Reduce long-term employee cost.

                                                  Avoid compulsory separation where possible.

                                                  Definition - Voluntary Retirement Scheme (VRS): VRS is an arrangement under which eligible employees voluntarily retire before normal retirement in return for specified benefits.
                                                  Definition - Golden Handshake: Golden handshake is a comparatively attractive financial package offered for voluntary early separation.

                                                  Objectives

                                                  • Reduce surplus workforce.
                                                  • Support restructuring and technology change.
                                                  • Reduce long-term employee cost.
                                                  • Avoid compulsory separation where possible.

                                                  Risks

                                                  • Loss of valuable experience.
                                                  • Low morale among remaining employees.
                                                  • Possibility that high performers leave.
                                                  • Large immediate financial outflow.
                                                  Suspension
                                                  SIMPLE MEANING
                                                  Suspension temporarily prevents an employee from performing duties, often pending investigation or disciplinary proceedings. It is not automatically the same as termination.
                                                  EASY DIAGRAM
                                                  Good Practice
                                                  →
                                                  Follow applicable law, standing orders and policy.
                                                  →
                                                  Provide written reasons and due process.
                                                  →
                                                  Complete the inquiry without unnecessary delay.
                                                  →
                                                  Maintain confidentiality and fairness.
                                                  SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                  S = Suspension
                                                  KEY POINTS
                                                  1. Good Practice
                                                  2. Follow applicable law, standing orders and policy.
                                                  3. Provide written reasons and due process.
                                                  4. Complete the inquiry without unnecessary delay.
                                                  5. Maintain confidentiality and fairness.
                                                  SIMPLE EXAMPLE

                                                  Discipline
                                                  SIMPLE MEANING
                                                  Discipline is orderly behaviour and compliance with organizational standards and rules.
                                                  EASY DIAGRAM
                                                  Positive vs Negative Discipline
                                                  →
                                                  Encourages self-control and understanding — Relies mainly on fear and punishment
                                                  →
                                                  Uses communication, counselling and correction — Uses warnings and penalties
                                                  →
                                                  Focuses on improvement — Focuses on offence
                                                  →
                                                  Builds long-term commitment — May create resentment if unfair
                                                  SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                  C-I-F-P-R: Clear rules, Investigate, Fair hearing, Proportionate action, Record
                                                  KEY POINTS
                                                  1. Positive vs Negative Discipline
                                                  2. Encourages self-control and understanding — Relies mainly on fear and punishment
                                                  3. Uses communication, counselling and correction — Uses warnings and penalties
                                                  4. Focuses on improvement — Focuses on offence
                                                  5. Builds long-term commitment — May create resentment if unfair
                                                  6. Principles of Disciplinary Action
                                                  7. Clear rules and prior communication.
                                                  8. Prompt and impartial investigation.
                                                  9. Opportunity for the employee to explain.
                                                  10. Consistency and proportionality.
                                                  SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                                  An employee repeatedly arrives late; the manager counsels, warns and records the action fairly.

                                                  Principles of Disciplinary Action

                                                  • Clear rules and prior communication.
                                                  • Prompt and impartial investigation.
                                                  • Opportunity for the employee to explain.
                                                  • Consistency and proportionality.
                                                  • Progressive action where appropriate.
                                                  • Proper records.
                                                  • Right to appeal or review.
                                                  Employee Grievance
                                                  SIMPLE MEANING
                                                  A grievance is an employee's feeling or complaint of dissatisfaction relating to employment, whether expressed or unexpressed, valid or perceived.
                                                  EASY DIAGRAM
                                                  Common Causes
                                                  →
                                                  Pay, incentives or benefits.
                                                  →
                                                  Workload, shift or working conditions.
                                                  →
                                                  Supervisor behaviour.
                                                  →
                                                  Promotion, transfer or appraisal.
                                                  SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                  P-W-S-P-L: Pay, Work, Supervisor, Promotion, Leave
                                                  KEY POINTS
                                                  1. Common Causes
                                                  2. Pay, incentives or benefits.
                                                  3. Workload, shift or working conditions.
                                                  4. Supervisor behaviour.
                                                  5. Promotion, transfer or appraisal.
                                                  6. Leave and attendance.
                                                  7. Discrimination, harassment or unfair treatment.
                                                  8. Job role and safety.
                                                  9. Disciplinary action.
                                                  SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                                  An employee complains that overtime payment was not included in salary.

                                                  UNIT 4 - QUICK REVISION
                                                  TopicKey Point
                                                  CompensationTotal financial and non-financial return
                                                  EquityInternal + external + individual fairness
                                                  Direct paySalary, wage, bonus and incentive
                                                  Indirect payBenefits and services
                                                  IncentiveReward linked to performance
                                                  Fringe benefitIndirect benefit in addition to salary
                                                  SeparationEnd of employment relationship
                                                  VRSVoluntary early retirement with a package
                                                  GrievanceEmployee dissatisfaction requiring resolution

                                                  Q1. Explain compensation management, its objectives and process.

                                                  Introduction: Compensation management designs and administers fair, competitive and affordable employee rewards.

                                                  1. Objectives: Attract, retain and motivate employees while maintaining equity and cost control.
                                                  2. Compensation philosophy: Decide how the organization wishes to position pay.
                                                  3. Job analysis: Understand duties and requirements.
                                                  4. Job evaluation: Determine relative internal job value.
                                                  5. Market survey: Study external pay levels.
                                                  6. Pay structure: Create grades and salary ranges.
                                                  7. Incentives and benefits: Link rewards with performance and employee needs.
                                                  8. Communication: Explain the system clearly.
                                                  9. Review: Check cost, fairness, competitiveness and effectiveness.

                                                  Conclusion: A sound compensation system balances employee expectations, market conditions and organizational ability to pay.

                                                  Q2. Explain financial and non-financial incentives and fringe benefits.

                                                  Introduction: Organizations use a combination of direct pay, variable rewards and non-financial recognition to motivate employees.

                                                  1. Financial incentives: Piece rate, commission, bonus, profit sharing, gain sharing, team incentive and stock options.
                                                  2. Non-financial incentives: Recognition, promotion, autonomy, participation, meaningful work and learning.
                                                  3. Fringe benefits: Insurance, leave, retirement, transport, canteen, wellness and employee services.
                                                  4. Design principles: Rewards should be fair, measurable, timely, understandable and aligned with quality and ethics.
                                                  5. Importance: The right mix improves motivation, retention, security and employer attractiveness.

                                                  Conclusion: Money is important, but sustainable motivation also depends on recognition, growth, fairness and the work environment.

                                                  Q3. Explain employee grievance and the grievance redressal procedure.

                                                  Introduction: A grievance is employee dissatisfaction connected with employment. A formal procedure helps resolve it fairly and prevents conflict.

                                                  1. Causes: Pay, supervisor behaviour, promotion, workload, leave, conditions, safety or unfair treatment.
                                                  2. First step: Employee raises the issue with the immediate supervisor.
                                                  3. Second step: Department head or HR investigates facts and discusses solutions.
                                                  4. Third step: Grievance committee or senior management reviews unresolved cases.
                                                  5. Appeal: The employee may use an appeal or voluntary arbitration mechanism where available.
                                                  6. Principles: Confidentiality, time limits, documentation, impartiality and no retaliation.
                                                  7. Follow-up: Confirm implementation and remove the root cause.

                                                  Conclusion: A credible grievance process strengthens trust, discipline and employee relations.

                                                  Q4. Explain major components of salary.

                                                  2 Marks5 Marks10/15 Marks

                                                  2 Marks Answer

                                                  Components of Salary explained in easy words using the points below. A wage differential is a difference in pay between jobs, employees, industries, regions or skill groups.

                                                  1. Basic salary/wage — Core fixed payment for the job.
                                                  2. Dearness allowance — Allowance intended to offset changes in cost of living.

                                                  Shortcut: B-A-B-I-B: Basic, Allowances, Bonus, Incentive, Benefits | WSD = Wage, Salary, Differentials

                                                  5 Marks Answer

                                                  Components of Salary explained in easy words using the points below. A wage differential is a difference in pay between jobs, employees, industries, regions or skill groups.

                                                  Basic salary/wage — Core fixed payment for the job.
                                                  →
                                                  Dearness allowance — Allowance intended to offset changes in cost of living.
                                                  →
                                                  House rent allowance — Support toward housing cost.
                                                  →
                                                  Other allowances — Travel, education, shift, location or special allowances.
                                                  →
                                                  Bonus — Additional payment based on law, profit or performance.
                                                  →
                                                  Reasons for Differentials
                                                  1. Basic salary/wage — Core fixed payment for the job.
                                                  2. Dearness allowance — Allowance intended to offset changes in cost of living.
                                                  3. House rent allowance — Support toward housing cost.
                                                  4. Other allowances — Travel, education, shift, location or special allowances.
                                                  5. Bonus — Additional payment based on law, profit or performance.

                                                  Example: Monthly pay may include basic salary, HRA, allowances, incentive and deductions.

                                                  Shortcut: B-A-B-I-B: Basic, Allowances, Bonus, Incentive, Benefits | WSD = Wage, Salary, Differentials

                                                  10/15 Marks Answer

                                                  Components of Salary explained in easy words using the points below. A wage differential is a difference in pay between jobs, employees, industries, regions or skill groups.

                                                  Basic salary/wage — Core fixed payment for the job.
                                                  →
                                                  Dearness allowance — Allowance intended to offset changes in cost of living.
                                                  →
                                                  House rent allowance — Support toward housing cost.
                                                  →
                                                  Other allowances — Travel, education, shift, location or special allowances.
                                                  →
                                                  Bonus — Additional payment based on law, profit or performance.
                                                  →
                                                  Reasons for Differentials
                                                  1. Basic salary/wage — Core fixed payment for the job.
                                                  2. Dearness allowance — Allowance intended to offset changes in cost of living.
                                                  3. House rent allowance — Support toward housing cost.
                                                  4. Other allowances — Travel, education, shift, location or special allowances.
                                                  5. Bonus — Additional payment based on law, profit or performance.
                                                  6. Incentive/variable pay — Payment linked to results, output or targets.
                                                  7. Benefits/perquisites — Insurance, retirement, leave, vehicle, meals or other facilities.
                                                  8. Deductions — Authorized deductions such as tax or employee contributions.
                                                  9. Reasons for Differentials

                                                  Example: Monthly pay may include basic salary, HRA, allowances, incentive and deductions.

                                                  Conclusion: The topic helps the organization use people effectively and achieve better results.

                                                  Q8. Explain forms of employee separation.

                                                  2 Marks5 Marks10/15 Marks

                                                  2 Marks Answer

                                                  Employee separation is the ending of the employment relationship between an employee and the organization.

                                                  1. Forms of Separation
                                                  2. Resignation — Employee voluntarily leaves the organization.

                                                  Shortcut: R-R-T-L-D: Resignation, Retirement, Termination, Layoff, Dismissal | S = Suspension

                                                  5 Marks Answer

                                                  Employee separation is the ending of the employment relationship between an employee and the organization.

                                                  Resignation / Retirement / Termination / Layoff
                                                  →
                                                  Exit process
                                                  →
                                                  Final settlement
                                                  →
                                                  Good Practice
                                                  →
                                                  Follow applicable law, standing orders and policy.
                                                  →
                                                  Provide written reasons and due process.
                                                  1. Forms of Separation
                                                  2. Resignation — Employee voluntarily leaves the organization.
                                                  3. Retirement — Employment ends after reaching retirement age or service conditions.
                                                  4. Voluntary Retirement Scheme (VRS) — Employee voluntarily accepts an organization-sponsored early-exit package.
                                                  5. Termination — Employer ends employment according to contract, policy or law.

                                                  Example: An employee may leave through resignation, retirement or termination.

                                                  Shortcut: R-R-T-L-D: Resignation, Retirement, Termination, Layoff, Dismissal | S = Suspension

                                                  10/15 Marks Answer

                                                  Employee separation is the ending of the employment relationship between an employee and the organization. Suspension temporarily prevents an employee from performing duties, often pending investigation or disciplinary proceedings. It is not automatically the same as termination.

                                                  Resignation / Retirement / Termination / Layoff
                                                  →
                                                  Exit process
                                                  →
                                                  Final settlement
                                                  →
                                                  Good Practice
                                                  →
                                                  Follow applicable law, standing orders and policy.
                                                  →
                                                  Provide written reasons and due process.
                                                  1. Forms of Separation
                                                  2. Resignation — Employee voluntarily leaves the organization.
                                                  3. Retirement — Employment ends after reaching retirement age or service conditions.
                                                  4. Voluntary Retirement Scheme (VRS) — Employee voluntarily accepts an organization-sponsored early-exit package.
                                                  5. Termination — Employer ends employment according to contract, policy or law.
                                                  6. Dismissal — Employment is ended as disciplinary punishment for serious misconduct.
                                                  7. Discharge — Employee is released for reasons other than major misconduct, subject to applicable rules.
                                                  8. Layoff — Temporary inability of the employer to provide work for specified reasons.
                                                  9. Retrenchment — Termination due to surplus workforce or restructuring, subject to law.

                                                  Example: An employee may leave through resignation, retirement or termination.

                                                  Conclusion: The topic helps the organization use people effectively and achieve better results.

                                                  Shortcut: R-R-T-L-D: Resignation, Retirement, Termination, Layoff, Dismissal | S = Suspension

                                                  VRS is an arrangement under which eligible employees voluntarily retire before normal retirement in return for specified benefits.

                                                  1. Reduce surplus workforce.
                                                  2. Support restructuring and technology change.

                                                  Shortcut: VRS = Voluntary exit + Retirement package

                                                  1. Internal equity: Comparable jobs receive comparable pay.
                                                  2. External competitiveness: Pay is reasonable compared with the labour market.

                                                  Shortcut: F-A-I-R: Fair, Affordable, Internal/market fit, Rule-compliant | S = Suspension

                                                        Definition - HRIS: A Human Resource Information System is an integrated system of people, processes, data and technology used to collect, store, process and report HR information.
                                                        Simple Meaning: HRIS = HR data + HR process + technology.
                                                        HRIS Structure Input: Employee and job data ↓ Processing: HR rules, calculations and workflows ↓ Database: Integrated employee records ↓ Output: Reports, dashboards and decisions
                                                        Need and Objectives of HRIS

                                                        →
                                                        Reduce duplication and paperwork.
                                                        →
                                                        Support payroll, attendance and benefits administration.
                                                        →
                                                        Improve workforce reporting and legal compliance.

                                                        OnboardingDocuments, tasks, induction and approvals
                                                        Time and attendanceShift, leave, overtime and attendance records
                                                        PayrollSalary processing, deductions and statements
                                                        PerformanceGoals, reviews, feedback and development plans
                                                        LearningCourses, enrolment, assessments and training history
                                                        Compensation and benefitsSalary review, incentives and benefit enrolment
                                                        Talent and successionSkills, potential, career and successor pools
                                                        Employee self-serviceProfile updates, leave requests, payslips and documents
                                                        AnalyticsHeadcount, turnover, diversity, cost and performance dashboards

                                                        Faster processing — Implementation cost
                                                        →
                                                        Accurate and consistent records — Data quality problems
                                                        →
                                                        Reduced paperwork — Privacy and security risk
                                                        →
                                                        Better reports and decisions — Employee resistance
                                                        →
                                                        Employee self-service — Need for training and support

                                                        Human Resource Accounting (HRA)
                                                        SIMPLE MEANING
                                                        HRA tries to show that people create economic value and that spending on people is often an investment.
                                                        EASY DIAGRAM
                                                        HR investment
                                                        →
                                                        Measure cost/value
                                                        →
                                                        Report
                                                        →
                                                        Management decision
                                                        SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                        C-R-O-P: Cost, Replacement, Opportunity, Present value
                                                        KEY POINTS
                                                        1. Objectives of HRA
                                                        2. Provide information about investment in people.
                                                        3. Support decisions on recruitment, training and development.
                                                        4. Measure changes in human-resource value.
                                                        5. Improve planning and control of human resources.
                                                        6. Increase management attention to employee capability.
                                                        7. Supplement traditional financial information.

                                                        Introduction: Human Resource Accounting (HRA) is an important part of HRM because it helps an organization manage employees in a planned and fair way.

                                                        Definition: HRA tries to show that people create economic value and that spending on people is often an investment.

                                                        HR investment
                                                        →
                                                        Measure cost/value
                                                        →
                                                        Report
                                                        →
                                                        Management decision
                                                        1. Objectives of HRA
                                                        2. Provide information about investment in people.
                                                        3. Support decisions on recruitment, training and development.
                                                        4. Measure changes in human-resource value.
                                                        5. Improve planning and control of human resources.
                                                        6. Increase management attention to employee capability.
                                                        7. Supplement traditional financial information.

                                                        Example: A company records recruitment and training investment to study the value of its workforce.

                                                        Conclusion: Therefore, human resource accounting (hra) helps improve employee effectiveness and organizational performance.

                                                        Show full explanation from the earlier HTML
                                                        Definition - Human Resource Accounting: Human Resource Accounting is the process of identifying, measuring and reporting investment in and value of human resources.
                                                        Simple Meaning: HRA tries to show that people create economic value and that spending on people is often an investment.

                                                        Objectives of HRA

                                                        • Provide information about investment in people.
                                                        • Support decisions on recruitment, training and development.
                                                        • Measure changes in human-resource value.
                                                        • Improve planning and control of human resources.
                                                        • Increase management attention to employee capability.
                                                        • Supplement traditional financial information.
                                                        Advantages and Limitations of HRA
                                                        SIMPLE MEANING
                                                        Advantages and Limitations of HRA explained in easy words using the points below.
                                                        EASY DIAGRAM
                                                        Highlights value of people — No universally accepted method
                                                        →
                                                        Supports HR investment decisions — Human value is difficult to measure
                                                        →
                                                        Encourages long-term development — Assumptions may be subjective
                                                        →
                                                        Helps compare HR cost and benefit — Employees cannot be owned like physical assets
                                                        →
                                                        May support planning and control — Employee tenure and future performance are uncertain
                                                        SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                        ALH = Advantages, Limitations, HRA

                                                        2. Supports HR investment decisions — Human value is difficult to measure
                                                        3. Encourages long-term development — Assumptions may be subjective
                                                        4. Helps compare HR cost and benefit — Employees cannot be owned like physical assets
                                                        5. May support planning and control — Employee tenure and future performance are uncertain

                                                        Methods of Human Resource Accounting
                                                        SIMPLE MEANING
                                                        Methods of Human Resource Accounting explained in easy words using the points below.
                                                        EASY DIAGRAM
                                                        HR investment
                                                        →
                                                        Measure cost/value
                                                        →
                                                        Report
                                                        →
                                                        Management decision
                                                        SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                        Explain the method, one advantage and one limitation. Do not present HRA values as perfectly precise.
                                                        KEY POINTS
                                                        1. Historical cost method — Capitalizes actual recruitment, selection, training and development costs.
                                                        2. Replacement cost method — Estimates cost of replacing existing employees with equivalent talent.
                                                        3. Opportunity cost method — Values scarce employees according to their best alternative internal use.
                                                        4. Present value of future earnings — Estimates present value of expected future earnings over service life.
                                                        5. Economic value method — Estimates future contribution or benefits attributable to employees.
                                                        SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                                        A company records recruitment and training investment to study the value of its workforce.
                                                        Write: Definition + 2 points

                                                        1. Historical cost method — Capitalizes actual recruitment, selection, training and development costs.
                                                        2. Replacement cost method — Estimates cost of replacing existing employees with equivalent talent.

                                                        Show full explanation from the earlier HTML
                                                        Definition - HR Audit: An HR audit is a systematic examination of HR policies, practices, records, systems and outcomes to assess compliance, effectiveness and alignment.
                                                        Simple Meaning: HR audit checks whether HR is doing the right things and doing them correctly.

                                                        Objectives

                                                        • Check legal and policy compliance.
                                                        • Evaluate effectiveness of HR functions.
                                                        • Identify gaps, risks and duplication.
                                                        • Improve HR policies and procedures.
                                                        • Align HR practices with business strategy.
                                                        • Improve records, controls and accountability.
                                                        • Benchmark and promote continuous improvement.
                                                        Scope of HR Audit
                                                        SIMPLE MEANING
                                                        Scope of HR Audit explained in easy words using the points below.
                                                        EASY DIAGRAM
                                                        HR strategy and organization — Roles, staffing, HR plan and business alignment
                                                        →
                                                        Recruitment and selection — Job documents, sourcing, fairness and records
                                                        →
                                                        Training and development — Needs, delivery, cost and effectiveness
                                                        →
                                                        Performance management — Goals, ratings, feedback and appeals
                                                        →
                                                        Compensation and benefits — Pay equity, administration and controls
                                                        SHORTCUT / TRICK
                                                        SHA = Scope, HR, Audit
                                                        KEY POINTS
                                                        1. HR strategy and organization — Roles, staffing, HR plan and business alignment
                                                        2. Recruitment and selection — Job documents, sourcing, fairness and records
                                                        3. Training and development — Needs, delivery, cost and effectiveness
                                                        4. Performance management — Goals, ratings, feedback and appeals
                                                        5. Compensation and benefits — Pay equity, administration and controls
                                                        6. Employee relations — Grievances, discipline, engagement and communication
                                                        7. Compliance and records — Policies, statutory records, privacy and documentation
                                                        8. HRIS and analytics — Access, security, data quality and reporting
                                                        9. Health, safety and welfare — Risk controls, incidents and employee services
                                                        10. Separation — Exit approvals, final settlement and exit data
                                                        SIMPLE EXAMPLE
                                                        HR checks whether recruitment records, pay practices and grievance procedures follow policy.

                                                        Scope of HR Audit explained in easy words using the points below.

                                                        1. HR strategy and organization — Roles, staffing, HR plan and business alignment
                                                        2. Recruitment and selection — Job documents, sourcing, fairness and records
                                                        Write: Meaning + Diagram + 5 points + Example

                                                        5. Compensation and benefits — Pay equity, administration and controls

                                                              Definition - HR Shared Services: HR shared services centralize common HR transactions and support activities so that multiple business units receive standardized, efficient service.
                                                              Simple Meaning: Instead of every department separately processing leave, payroll or employee queries, one shared HR service centre handles them.
                                                              Common HR Operating Model HR Business Partners: Business and people strategy Centres of Expertise: Specialist policy and design Shared Services: Standard transactions and employee support Digital Self-Service: Employee and manager access

                                                              UNIT 5 - QUICK REVISION
                                                              TopicKey Point
                                                              HRISIntegrated HR data, process and technology system
                                                              E-HRMDigital delivery of HR services
                                                              HRAMeasurement and reporting of investment/value of people
                                                              HR auditSystematic check of HR compliance and effectiveness
                                                              Shared servicesCentralized delivery of common HR transactions
                                                              HR analyticsUse HR data to improve people decisions
                                                              Modern trendsAI, hybrid work, gig workforce, employee experience, DEI and Green HRM

                                                              Q1. Explain HRIS, its need, uses, advantages and limitations.

                                                              Introduction: HRIS integrates HR data, processes and technology to improve HR administration and decision making.

                                                              1. Need: Central records, faster transactions, better control and reliable workforce information.
                                                              2. Core uses: Employee records, payroll, attendance, recruitment, learning, performance and benefits.
                                                              3. Decision support: Reports and dashboards support planning and analysis.
                                                              4. Employee self-service: Employees update data, apply leave and access documents.
                                                              5. Advantages: Speed, accuracy, consistency, reduced paperwork and improved reporting.
                                                              6. Limitations: Cost, poor data, privacy risk, resistance, vendor dependence and need for training.
                                                              7. Implementation: Requirements, process mapping, system selection, data migration, testing, training and review.

                                                              Conclusion: HRIS creates value only when accurate data, sound processes, security and user adoption are managed together.

                                                              Q2. Explain Human Resource Accounting and its methods.

                                                              Introduction: Human Resource Accounting attempts to identify, measure and report investment in and value of human resources.

                                                              1. Objectives: Support HR investment decisions, planning and management attention to human capability.
                                                              2. Historical cost: Uses actual recruitment, training and development cost.
                                                              3. Replacement cost: Uses estimated cost of replacing existing employees.
                                                              4. Opportunity cost: Uses value of the best alternative internal use of scarce talent.
                                                              5. Present value method: Discounts expected future earnings or benefits.
                                                              6. Advantages: Highlights people as value-creating resources and supports long-term decisions.
                                                              7. Limitations: Human value is uncertain, methods differ and assumptions can be subjective.

                                                              Conclusion: HRA is best used as supplementary management information rather than as an exact measure of human worth.

                                                              Q3. Explain HR Audit and its process.

                                                              Introduction: HR audit systematically evaluates HR policies, practices, records and results for compliance and effectiveness.

                                                              1. Scope and standards: Define what will be audited and the criteria.
                                                              2. Evidence collection: Review records, reports, policies, interviews and employee feedback.
                                                              3. Evaluation: Compare practice with legal, policy and benchmark requirements.
                                                              4. Risk identification: Classify gaps according to seriousness and impact.
                                                              5. Reporting: Present strengths, findings and practical recommendations.
                                                              6. Action plan: Assign responsibility and deadlines.
                                                              7. Follow-up: Confirm correction and continuous improvement.
                                                              8. Benefits: Better compliance, efficiency, accountability and strategic alignment.

                                                              Conclusion: An HR audit is not merely fault finding; it is a structured tool for improving the complete HR system.

                                                              Q4. Explain HR Shared Services, its benefits and implementation issues.

                                                              Introduction: HR shared services centralize routine HR processes for multiple business units while specialists and HR business partners focus on expertise and strategy.

                                                              1. Coverage: Employee queries, records, payroll support, leave, letters and standard transactions.
                                                              2. Benefits: Lower duplication, standardization, consistent service, better technology and reporting.
                                                              3. Service model: Shared services work with centres of expertise, HR business partners and digital self-service.
                                                              4. Issues: Resistance, loss of local touch, unclear roles, poor technology and service-quality concerns.
                                                              5. Success conditions: Standard processes, service-level agreements, governance, capable staff and change management.

                                                              Conclusion: Shared services succeed when efficiency is balanced with responsive and human-centred employee service.

                                                              2. Accurate and consistent records — Data quality problems

                                                              Q4. Define Human Resource Accounting and explain its objectives, advantages and limitations.

                                                              2 Marks5 Marks10/15 Marks

                                                              2 Marks Answer

                                                              HRA tries to show that people create economic value and that spending on people is often an investment. Methods of Human Resource Accounting explained in easy words using the points below.

                                                              1. Objectives of HRA
                                                              2. Provide information about investment in people.

                                                              Shortcut: C-R-O-P: Cost, Replacement, Opportunity, Present value | Explain the method, one advantage and one limitation. Do not present HRA values as perfectly precise.

                                                              5 Marks Answer

                                                              HRA tries to show that people create economic value and that spending on people is often an investment. Methods of Human Resource Accounting explained in easy words using the points below.

                                                              HR investment
                                                              →
                                                              Measure cost/value
                                                              →
                                                              Report
                                                              →
                                                              Management decision
                                                              →
                                                              Faster processing — Implementation cost
                                                              →
                                                              Accurate and consistent records — Data quality problems
                                                              1. Objectives of HRA
                                                              2. Provide information about investment in people.
                                                              3. Support decisions on recruitment, training and development.
                                                              4. Measure changes in human-resource value.
                                                              5. Improve planning and control of human resources.

                                                              Example: A company records recruitment and training investment to study the value of its workforce.

                                                              Shortcut: C-R-O-P: Cost, Replacement, Opportunity, Present value | Explain the method, one advantage and one limitation. Do not present HRA values as perfectly precise.

                                                              10/15 Marks Answer

                                                              HRA tries to show that people create economic value and that spending on people is often an investment. Methods of Human Resource Accounting explained in easy words using the points below.

                                                              HR investment
                                                              →
                                                              Measure cost/value
                                                              →
                                                              Report
                                                              →
                                                              Management decision
                                                              →
                                                              Faster processing — Implementation cost
                                                              →

                                                              1. Objectives of HRA
                                                              2. Provide information about investment in people.
                                                              3. Support decisions on recruitment, training and development.
                                                              4. Measure changes in human-resource value.
                                                              5. Improve planning and control of human resources.
                                                              6. Increase management attention to employee capability.
                                                              7. Supplement traditional financial information.
                                                              8. Historical cost method — Capitalizes actual recruitment, selection, training and development costs.
                                                              9. Replacement cost method — Estimates cost of replacing existing employees with equivalent talent.

                                                              Example: A company records recruitment and training investment to study the value of its workforce.

                                                              Conclusion: The topic helps the organization use people effectively and achieve better results.

                                                              Q6. Define HR Audit and explain its objectives, scope and process.

                                                              1. HR strategy and organization — Roles, staffing, HR plan and business alignment
                                                              2. Recruitment and selection — Job documents, sourcing, fairness and records

                                                              Shortcut: SHA = Scope, HR, Audit | C-E-A: Compliance, Effectiveness, Alignment

                                                              MASTER ANSWER FORMULA

                                                              Definition → Diagram → Numbered points → Example → Conclusion

                                                              TopicShortcut
                                                              Functions of HRMPODC + PDCIMS = Planning, Organizing, Directing, Controlling + Procurement, Development, Compensation, Integration, Maintenance, Separation.
                                                              Human Resource Planning ProcessO-I-D-S-G-A-I-R = Objectives, Inventory, Demand, Supply, Gap, Action, Implement, Review.
                                                              Job AnalysisJA = Job, Analysis
                                                              Job Analysis ProcessP-S-C-V-W: Purpose, Select jobs, Collect data, Verify, Write JD/JS
                                                              Methods of Job AnalysisO-I-Q-D-C: Observation, Interview, Questionnaire, Diary, Conference
                                                              Uses of Job AnalysisUJA = Uses, Job, Analysis
                                                              SelectionUse the same job-related questions, a rating scale and trained interviewers to reduce bias.
                                                              Recruitment and SelectionV-S-A-S: Vacancy, Source, Attract, Screen
                                                              Performance Appraisal ProcessS-C-M-M-C-D-A-F = Standards, Communicate, Method, Measure, Compare, Discuss, Action, Follow-up.
                                                              Training Need Assessment (TNA)O-T-P: Organization, Task, Person
                                                              Evaluation of Training - Kirkpatrick ModelR-L-B-R = Reaction, Learning, Behaviour, Results.
                                                              Compensation Management ProcessA-E-S-P-C-R: Analyze, Evaluate jobs, Survey market, Pay structure, Communicate, Review
                                                              Grievance Redressal ProcedureS-H-C-A-F: Supervisor, HR, Committee, Appeal, Follow-up
                                                              Human Resource Information System (HRIS)D-P-T-R: Data, Process, Technology, Reports
                                                              HR Analytics - Basic FrameworkHR data must be accurate, relevant, secure, interpreted fairly and used with human judgment.
